The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 4 is 0.20. The other faces contribute 0.30 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.08	Incorrect	0.10	Incorrect	0.30	Incorrect	0.50	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 1.07. The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 5.00. The other faces contribute 5.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.00	Incorrect	1.67	Incorrect	5.00	Incorrect	10.00	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 6.00. The other faces contribute 6.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.00	Incorrect	2.00	Incorrect	6.00	Incorrect	12.00	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 0.05. The other faces contribute 0.45 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.08	Incorrect	0.40	Incorrect	0.45	Incorrect	0.50	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 12.00. The other faces contribute 12.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.00	Incorrect	4.00	Incorrect	12.00	Incorrect	24.00	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 4 is 5.00. The other faces contribute 5.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.00	Incorrect	1.67	Incorrect	5.00	Incorrect	10.00	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.67. The other faces contribute 9.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	1.61	Incorrect	8.33	Incorrect	9.00	Incorrect	9.67	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 0.08. The other faces contribute 0.75 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.14	Incorrect	0.67	Incorrect	0.75	Incorrect	0.83	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 5.00. The other faces contribute 5.00 in total. What is &chi;<sup>2</sup>? Round to two decimal places.</p>	0.00	Incorrect	1.67	Incorrect	5.00	Incorrect	10.00	Correct
